========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hi, yeah, thanks. Um, well, I guess what finally made me call the EAP—our Employee Assistance Program—was that every little bang in the office is just... it's really getting to me. Like, if a heavy file drawer shuts, or if someone drops a coffee mug, I just jump so hard. 

We actually have these really thick ceramic mugs in the breakroom, the ones with the old company logo? I've been telling the higher-ups we should switch to paper cups or insulated tumblers or something because my sales guys are always leaving them right on the edge of their desks. They get so animated on their calls, waving their hands around, and boom, it hits the floor. I supervise the outbound team, so I'm used to it being kind of a loud floor anyway, but... wait, what was I saying? 

Oh, right, the noise. Yeah, when something drops or bangs like that lately, I just jump out of my skin, and my chest keeps racing way, way longer than it should. It just takes me forever to calm down from it.

Yeah, um, actually, I do. It's not just the noise. Like, when I'm in the office now, I'm constantly... I don't know, checking things? Um, a sudden intense fear peaking in minutes? No, I don't think I've ever really had anything like that. I mean, the only thing that reaches a peak in minutes around my office is when the auto-dialer system crashes. I'm supposed to be monitoring my team's calls—they do this thing where they read the script too fast, and I've been trying to get them to slow down and really enunciate, you know? Because if you rush the pitch, the client just tunes out. I had this one guy last week, Greg, he blew through the intro so fast the guy on the other end thought he was a recording and hung up. I told Greg, 'Man, you gotta breathe.' But yeah, even when I'm trying to listen to their calls, I'm just constantly scanning the room, looking at the doors, checking who's behind me. It's exhausting.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a sudden feeling of intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: Um, a sudden intense fear peaking in minutes? No, I don't think I've ever really had anything like that. I mean, the only thing that reaches a peak in minutes around my office is when the auto-dialer system crashes. Seriously, if the dialer goes down, the whole floor just descends into absolute chaos in like, two minutes flat. I keep telling our IT department—especially this guy Steve, who always takes his lunch right at 1:00 PM no matter what is currently on fire—I'm like, Steve, you can't just clock out when the server is lagging. We lose so many good leads when that happens, and my guys work on commission, so they get super heated and start yelling across the room. It's a whole ordeal. \n\nBut, uh... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, the fear thing. Yeah, no, I don't get like a sudden wave of terror or anything like that. Just the being on edge and jumping at stuff.\n\nDoctor: Are you anxious all the time, even when you're not at work or in a situation that might be loud?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really all the time. Like, once I get home, I'm actually pretty okay. My apartment is usually pretty quiet. Well, except on Tuesdays when the garbage trucks come by. They do this massive recycling pickup and it clatters everywhere, but I know what it is, so it doesn't bother me as much. I actually had to call the city about them last month, though, because they kept leaving the empty bins right in the middle of my driveway. I was like, come on guys, I have to get to work. I drive this older Civic and it sits pretty low to the ground, so if I hit a plastic bin, it's definitely going to scratch the bumper. I just got it detailed, too, by this guy my buddy recommended. He did an amazing job on the upholstery, got these weird coffee stains out from the previous owner. I have no idea how you even spill coffee on the ceiling of a car, but he managed to scrub it right out... \n\nBut, uh, sorry. What were you asking? Oh, right, being anxious everywhere. No, I don't feel on edge when I'm just chilling at home or out running errands. Um, well, I guess... I mean, I don't really know if there's one specific thing causing it. It's just being there, I think. The whole environment. It's an open-plan office, which they said was going to foster "collaboration" when they remodeled it last year. But honestly, it's just a nightmare for trying to get any actual work done. There's no privacy at all. They put in these standing desks, which are supposed to be ergonomic, but the motors on them are so loud when people adjust them. And my desk is right by the breakroom, so I constantly hear the microwave beeping. We have this one guy, Todd, who microwaves fish at least twice a week. I've brought it up at the supervisor meetings—I'm like, guys, it's a basic office courtesy thing, we need a policy on pungent foods. But HR says we can't dictate what people eat for lunch. I get that, but when the whole floor smells like tilapia by 12:30, it really kills the vibe, you know? Plus, the fridge is always packed because people leave their Tupperware in there for weeks. I had to throw out a container of yogurt that expired in 2021 the other day. It was practically a science experiment. 

But, uh, wait... what was the question? Something about the office? Yeah, I don't really know what's causing the anxiety there, specifically. I just feel super on edge the whole time I'm in the building.

It's a whole ordeal. \n\nBut, uh... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, the fear thing. Yeah, no, I don't get like a sudden wave of terror or anything like that. Just the being on edge and jumping at stuff.\n\nDoctor: Are you anxious all the time, even when you're not at work or in a situation that might be loud?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really all the time. Like, once I get home, I'm actually pretty okay. My apartment is usually pretty quiet. Well, except on Tuesdays when the garbage trucks come by. They do this massive recycling pickup and it clatters everywhere, but I know what it is, so it doesn't bother me as much. I actually had to call the city about them last month, though, because they kept leaving the empty bins right in the middle of my driveway. I was like, come on guys, I have to get to work. I drive this older Civic and it sits pretty low to the ground, so if I hit a plastic bin, it's definitely going to scratch the bumper. I just got it detailed, too, by this guy my buddy recommended. He did an amazing job on the upholstery, got these weird coffee stains out from the previous owner. I have no idea how you even spill coffee on the ceiling of a car, but he managed to scrub it right out... \n\nBut, uh, sorry. What were you asking? Oh, right, being anxious everywhere. No, I don't feel on edge when I'm just chilling at home or out running errands. It's really just when I'm at the office or if there's a bunch of sudden noise.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything in particular that you think is causing you to feel anxious at the office?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I guess... I mean, I don't really know if there's one specific thing causing it. It's just being there, I think. The whole environment. It's an open-plan office, which they said was going to foster \"collaboration\" when they remodeled it last year. But honestly, it's just a nightmare for trying to get any actual work done. There's no privacy at all. They put in these standing desks, which are supposed to be ergonomic, but the motors on them are so loud when people adjust them. And my desk is right by the breakroom, so I constantly hear the microwave beeping. We have this one guy, Todd, who microwaves fish at least twice a week. I've brought it up at the supervisor meetings—I'm like, guys, it's a basic office courtesy thing, we need a policy on pungent foods. But HR says we can't dictate what people eat for lunch. I get that, but when the whole floor smells like tilapia by 12:30, it really kills the vibe, you know? Plus, the fridge is always packed because people leave their Tupperware in there for weeks. I had to throw out a container of yogurt that expired in 2021 the other day. It was practically a science experiment. \n\nBut, uh, wait... what was the question? Something about the office? Yeah, I don't really know what's causing the anxiety there, specifically. I just feel super on edge the whole time I'm in the building.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you worry about a wide range of things in your daily life, not just related to work or noise?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 27 / first_line_supervisor_of_non_retail_sales_worker / some_college\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, worry about a wide range of things? No, not really. I mean, I don't sit around stressing about everything under the sun. I pay my bills on time, mostly. Well, except for that one time last month when my auto-pay just mysteriously turned itself off for my internet bill. I use this provider, they're the only ones that service my building, and their customer service is just atrocious. I was on hold for like 45 minutes listening to this awful elevator jazz music. And when I finally got a hold of someone, the guy—I think his name was Gary—tried to tell me I owed a late fee. I was like, Gary, your system is the one that glitched! I had to escalate it to a manager just to get a $15 charge removed. It's the principle of the thing, you know? They just expect people to not notice and pay up. It's like how streaming services keep raising their prices by two dollars every few months hoping you won't cancel. I actually did cancel a bunch of them recently because I realized I was paying for like, four different platforms and only watching reruns of old sitcoms anyway. 

But, uh, wait... what did you ask me? Oh, right, worrying about everything. No, I don't really worry about random daily stuff. It's really just the being jumpy at the office, like I said.

Um, anxious when I'm alone with my thoughts? No, not really. When I'm just by myself, I'm usually fine. I actually kind of like the quiet. I try to read a lot when I'm alone. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, crowded places outside the office? No, not really. I mean, I go to the grocery store and stuff and I'm totally fine. Actually, speaking of the grocery store, I was at Trader Joe's this weekend, and it was an absolute zoo. You know how their parking lots are always ridiculously small? I swear it's part of their business model to make you feel like you've accomplished something just by getting a spot. Anyway, I was in line for like twenty minutes because they only had three registers open. The guy in front of me had an entire cart full of just frozen mandarin orange chicken and two-buck Chuck. I mean, I like the orange chicken as much as the next guy, but that's a lot of sodium. I usually try to stick to the fresh produce section, even though their avocados are always either rock hard or complete mush. There is literally no in-between. I tried making guacamole last Tuesday for Taco Tuesday, which I do with my roommate, but the avocados were so bad I ended up just making salsa instead. I have a pretty good salsa recipe, actually. It uses roasted tomatillos. 

But, uh, wait, what were we talking about? Crowded places? Yeah, no, I don't get anxious in crowds or public places. Just the office.

No, I don't think I've ever really had anything like that. I mean, the only thing that reaches a peak in minutes around my office is when the auto-dialer system crashes. Seriously, if the dialer goes down, the whole floor just descends into absolute chaos in like, two minutes flat. I keep telling our IT department—especially this guy Steve, who always takes his lunch right at 1:00 PM no matter what is currently on fire—I'm like, Steve, you can't just clock out when the server is lagging. We lose so many good leads when that happens, and my guys work on commission, so they get super heated and start yelling across the room. It's a whole ordeal. \n\nBut, uh... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, the fear thing. Yeah, no, I don't get like a sudden wave of terror or anything like that. Just the being on edge and jumping at stuff.\n\nDoctor: Are you anxious all the time, even when you're not at work or in a situation that might be loud?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really all the time. Like, once I get home, I'm actually pretty okay. My apartment is usually pretty quiet. Well, except on Tuesdays when the garbage trucks come by. They do this massive recycling pickup and it clatters everywhere, but I know what it is, so it doesn't bother me as much. I actually had to call the city about them last month, though, because they kept leaving the empty bins right in the middle of my driveway. I was like, come on guys, I have to get to work. I drive this older Civic and it sits pretty low to the ground, so if I hit a plastic bin, it's definitely going to scratch the bumper. I just got it detailed, too, by this guy my buddy recommended. He did an amazing job on the upholstery, got these weird coffee stains out from the previous owner. I have no idea how you even spill coffee on the ceiling of a car, but he managed to scrub it right out... \n\nBut, uh, sorry. What were you asking? Oh, right, being anxious everywhere. No, I don't feel on edge when I'm just chilling at home or out running errands. It's really just when I'm at the office or if there's a bunch of sudden noise.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything in particular that you think is causing you to feel anxious at the office?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I guess... I mean, I don't really know if there's one specific thing causing it. It's just being there, I think. The whole environment. It's an open-plan office, which they said was going to foster \"collaboration\" when they remodeled it last year. But honestly, it's just a nightmare for trying to get any actual work done. There's no privacy at all. They put in these standing desks, which are supposed to be ergonomic, but the motors on them are so loud when people adjust them. And my desk is right by the breakroom, so I constantly hear the microwave beeping. We have this one guy, Todd, who microwaves fish at least twice a week. I've brought it up at the supervisor meetings—I'm like, guys, it's a basic office courtesy thing, we need a policy on pungent foods. But HR says we can't dictate what people eat for lunch. I get that, but when the whole floor smells like tilapia by 12:30, it really kills the vibe, you know? Plus, the fridge is always packed because people leave their Tupperware in there for weeks. I had to throw out a container of yogurt that expired in 2021 the other day. It was practically a science experiment. \n\nBut, uh, wait... what was the question? Something about the office? Yeah, I don't really know what's causing the anxiety there, specifically. I just feel super on edge the whole time I'm in the building.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about a wide range of things in your daily life, not just related to work or noise?\n\nPatient: Um, worry about a wide range of things? No, not really. I mean, I don't sit around stressing about everything under the sun. I pay my bills on time, mostly. Well, except for that one time last month when my auto-pay just mysteriously turned itself off for my internet bill. I use this provider, they're the only ones that service my building, and their customer service is just atrocious. I was on hold for like 45 minutes listening to this awful elevator jazz music. And when I finally got a hold of someone, the guy—I think his name was Gary—tried to tell me I owed a late fee. I was like, Gary, your system is the one that glitched! I had to escalate it to a manager just to get a $15 charge removed. It's the principle of the thing, you know? They just expect people to not notice and pay up. It's like how streaming services keep raising their prices by two dollars every few months hoping you won't cancel. I actually did cancel a bunch of them recently because I realized I was paying for like, four different platforms and only watching reruns of old sitcoms anyway. \n\nBut, uh, wait... what did you ask me? Oh, right, worrying about everything. No, I don't really worry about random daily stuff. It's really just the being jumpy at the office, like I said.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're alone with your thoughts, outside of work or noisy situations?\n\nPatient: Um, anxious when I'm alone with my thoughts? No, not really. When I'm just by myself, I'm usually fine. I actually kind of like the quiet. I try to read a lot when I'm alone. I just picked up this sci-fi series my brother recommended. It's about this colony on Mars that loses contact with Earth, and the politics of how they survive. It's super dense, though, like the author spends three pages just describing the hydroponic tomato farms. I tried growing tomatoes on my balcony last summer, actually. Total disaster. I bought these heirloom seeds online, got the special soil, even bought a little watering can that looked like a flamingo. But the squirrels in my neighborhood are just absolutely ruthless. They would wait until the tomatoes were perfectly red and ripe, and then just take one bite out of them and leave them on the railing to taunt me. I swear they were doing it on purpose. I tried putting cayenne pepper on the dirt because I read online that deters them, but then it rained and washed it all away, and I just gave up and went back to buying them at the farmer's market. There's this guy there who sells the best cherry tomatoes, anyway. \n\nBut, uh, sorry, what was I saying? Oh, right, being alone with my thoughts. Yeah, no, I don't really get anxious when I'm just sitting by myself or anything like that. It's just the work stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're in other crowded or public places, outside of your office?\n\nPatient: Um, crowded places outside the office? No, not really. I mean, I go to the grocery store and stuff and I'm totally fine. Actually, speaking of the grocery store, I was at Trader Joe's this weekend, and it was an absolute zoo. You know how their parking lots are always ridiculously small? I swear it's part of their business model to make you feel like you've accomplished something just by getting a spot. Anyway, I was in line for like twenty minutes because they only had three registers open. The guy in front of me had an entire cart full of just frozen mandarin orange chicken and two-buck Chuck. I mean, I like the orange chicken as much as the next guy, but that's a lot of sodium. I usually try to stick to the fresh produce section, even though their avocados are always either rock hard or complete mush. There is literally no in-between. I tried making guacamole last Tuesday for Taco Tuesday, which I do with my roommate, but the avocados were so bad I ended up just making salsa instead. I have a pretty good salsa recipe, actually. It uses roasted tomatillos. \n\nBut, uh, wait, what were we talking about? Crowded places? Yeah, no, I don't get anxious in crowds or public places. Just the office.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Um, upsetting? Yeah, I guess so. This has all been going on for about two weeks, ever since this late-night confrontation I had with my regional boss. It was a whole thing. He came in way past normal hours, which he never does, and just started tearing into me about the quarterly numbers. And the thing is, the numbers weren't even finalized yet! Our software system, Salesforce, had been doing this weird update thing all week where it would duplicate leads and then randomly delete the notes attached to them. I spent like three hours on the phone with IT on Wednesday, and the IT guy—I think his name was Kevin, or maybe Kyle, he had this really thick Boston accent—was telling me that it was a known issue with the new patch they rolled out. They always roll out these patches without testing them properly, I swear. It's like when my phone forced an update overnight and suddenly all my alarm sounds changed to this weird harp music. I was late to work because who wakes up to a harp? It's too soothing. You need something jarring, like a buzzer or a rooster crowing. I actually used to live near a farm when I was a kid, and they had this rooster that would go off at 4:30 AM every single day. My dad hated that bird. He used to joke about making chicken soup out of it. 

But, uh, anyway... what was the question? Oh, right, something upsetting. Yeah, it was that confrontation with my boss two weeks ago. That's when all this jumpiness started.

I have no idea how you even spill coffee on the ceiling of a car, but he managed to scrub it right out... \n\nBut, uh, sorry. What were you asking? Oh, right, being anxious everywhere. It's really just when I'm at the office or if there's a bunch of sudden noise.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything in particular that you think is causing you to feel anxious at the office?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I guess... I mean, I don't really know if there's one specific thing causing it. It's just being there, I think. The whole environment. It's an open-plan office, which they said was going to foster \"collaboration\" when they remodeled it last year. But honestly, it's just a nightmare for trying to get any actual work done. There's no privacy at all. They put in these standing desks, which are supposed to be ergonomic, but the motors on them are so loud when people adjust them. And my desk is right by the breakroom, so I constantly hear the microwave beeping. We have this one guy, Todd, who microwaves fish at least twice a week. I've brought it up at the supervisor meetings—I'm like, guys, it's a basic office courtesy thing, we need a policy on pungent foods. But HR says we can't dictate what people eat for lunch. I get that, but when the whole floor smells like tilapia by 12:30, it really kills the vibe, you know? Plus, the fridge is always packed because people leave their Tupperware in there for weeks. I had to throw out a container of yogurt that expired in 2021 the other day. It was practically a science experiment. \n\nBut, uh, wait... what was the question? Something about the office? Yeah, I don't really know what's causing the anxiety there, specifically. I just feel super on edge the whole time I'm in the building.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about a wide range of things in your daily life, not just related to work or noise?\n\nPatient: Um, worry about a wide range of things? No, not really. I mean, I don't sit around stressing about everything under the sun. I pay my bills on time, mostly. Well, except for that one time last month when my auto-pay just mysteriously turned itself off for my internet bill. I use this provider, they're the only ones that service my building, and their customer service is just atrocious. I was on hold for like 45 minutes listening to this awful elevator jazz music. And when I finally got a hold of someone, the guy—I think his name was Gary—tried to tell me I owed a late fee. I was like, Gary, your system is the one that glitched! I had to escalate it to a manager just to get a $15 charge removed. It's the principle of the thing, you know? They just expect people to not notice and pay up. It's like how streaming services keep raising their prices by two dollars every few months hoping you won't cancel. I actually did cancel a bunch of them recently because I realized I was paying for like, four different platforms and only watching reruns of old sitcoms anyway. \n\nBut, uh, wait... what did you ask me? Oh, right, worrying about everything. No, I don't really worry about random daily stuff. It's really just the being jumpy at the office, like I said.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're alone with your thoughts, outside of work or noisy situations?\n\nPatient: Um, anxious when I'm alone with my thoughts? No, not really. When I'm just by myself, I'm usually fine. I actually kind of like the quiet. I try to read a lot when I'm alone. I just picked up this sci-fi series my brother recommended. It's about this colony on Mars that loses contact with Earth, and the politics of how they survive. It's super dense, though, like the author spends three pages just describing the hydroponic tomato farms. I tried growing tomatoes on my balcony last summer, actually. Total disaster. I bought these heirloom seeds online, got the special soil, even bought a little watering can that looked like a flamingo. But the squirrels in my neighborhood are just absolutely ruthless. They would wait until the tomatoes were perfectly red and ripe, and then just take one bite out of them and leave them on the railing to taunt me. I swear they were doing it on purpose. I tried putting cayenne pepper on the dirt because I read online that deters them, but then it rained and washed it all away, and I just gave up and went back to buying them at the farmer's market. You know how their parking lots are always ridiculously small? I swear it's part of their business model to make you feel like you've accomplished something just by getting a spot. Anyway, I was in line for like twenty minutes because they only had three registers open. The guy in front of me had an entire cart full of just frozen mandarin orange chicken and two-buck Chuck. I mean, I like the orange chicken as much as the next guy, but that's a lot of sodium. I usually try to stick to the fresh produce section, even though their avocados are always either rock hard or complete mush. There is literally no in-between. I tried making guacamole last Tuesday for Taco Tuesday, which I do with my roommate, but the avocados were so bad I ended up just making salsa instead. I have a pretty good salsa recipe, actually. It uses roasted tomatillos. \n\nBut, uh, wait, what were we talking about? Crowded places? Yeah, no, I don't get anxious in crowds or public places. Just the office.\n\nDoctor: Has something specific and upsetting happened to you recently or in the past that you think might be contributing to how you're feeling now?\n\nPatient: Um, upsetting? Yeah, I guess so. This has all been going on for about two weeks, ever since this late-night confrontation I had with my regional boss. It was a whole thing. He came in way past normal hours, which he never does, and just started tearing into me about the quarterly numbers. And the thing is, the numbers weren't even finalized yet! Our software system, Salesforce, had been doing this weird update thing all week where it would duplicate leads and then randomly delete the notes attached to them. I spent like three hours on the phone with IT on Wednesday, and the IT guy—I think his name was Kevin, or maybe Kyle, he had this really thick Boston accent—was telling me that it was a known issue with the new patch they rolled out. They always roll out these patches without testing them properly, I swear. It's like when my phone forced an update overnight and suddenly all my alarm sounds changed to this weird harp music. I was late to work because who wakes up to a harp? It's too soothing. You need something jarring, like a buzzer or a rooster crowing. I actually used to live near a farm when I was a kid, and they had this rooster that would go off at 4:30 AM every single day. My dad hated that bird. He used to joke about making chicken soup out of it. \n\nBut, uh, anyway... what was the question? Oh, right, something upsetting. Yeah, it was that confrontation with my boss two weeks ago. That's when all this jumpiness started.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It's super dense, though, like the author spends three pages just describing the hydroponic tomato farms. I tried growing tomatoes on my balcony last summer, actually. Total disaster. I bought these heirloom seeds online, got the special soil, even bought a little watering can that looked like a flamingo. But the squirrels in my neighborhood are just absolutely ruthless. They would wait until the tomatoes were perfectly red and ripe, and then just take one bite out of them and leave them on the railing to taunt me. I swear they were doing it on purpose. I tried putting cayenne pepper on the dirt because I read online that deters them, but then it rained and washed it all away, and I just gave up and went back to buying them at the farmer's market. There's this guy there who sells the best cherry tomatoes, anyway. \n\nBut, uh, sorry, what was I saying? Oh, right, being alone with my thoughts. Yeah, no, I don't really get anxious when I'm just sitting by myself or anything like that. It's just the work stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're in other crowded or public places, outside of your office?\n\nPatient: Um, crowded places outside the office? No, not really. I mean, I go to the grocery store and stuff and I'm totally fine. Actually, speaking of the grocery store, I was at Trader Joe's this weekend, and it was an absolute zoo. You know how their parking lots are always ridiculously small? I swear it's part of their business model to make you feel like you've accomplished something just by getting a spot. Anyway, I was in line for like twenty minutes because they only had three registers open. The guy in front of me had an entire cart full of just frozen mandarin orange chicken and two-buck Chuck. I mean, I like the orange chicken as much as the next guy, but that's a lot of sodium. I usually try to stick to the fresh produce section, even though their avocados are always either rock hard or complete mush. There is literally no in-between. I tried making guacamole last Tuesday for Taco Tuesday, which I do with my roommate, but the avocados were so bad I ended up just making salsa instead. I have a pretty good salsa recipe, actually. It uses roasted tomatillos. \n\nBut, uh, wait, what were we talking about? Crowded places? Yeah, no, I don't get anxious in crowds or public places. Just the office.\n\nDoctor: Has something specific and upsetting happened to you recently or in the past that you think might be contributing to how you're feeling now?\n\nPatient: Um, upsetting? Yeah, I guess so. This has all been going on for about two weeks, ever since this late-night confrontation I had with my regional boss. It was a whole thing. He came in way past normal hours, which he never does, and just started tearing into me about the quarterly numbers. And the thing is, the numbers weren't even finalized yet! Our software system, Salesforce, had been doing this weird update thing all week where it would duplicate leads and then randomly delete the notes attached to them. I spent like three hours on the phone with IT on Wednesday, and the IT guy—I think his name was Kevin, or maybe Kyle, he had this really thick Boston accent—was telling me that it was a known issue with the new patch they rolled out. They always roll out these patches without testing them properly, I swear. It's like when my phone forced an update overnight and suddenly all my alarm sounds changed to this weird harp music. I was late to work because who wakes up to a harp? It's too soothing. You need something jarring, like a buzzer or a rooster crowing. I actually used to live near a farm when I was a kid, and they had this rooster that would go off at 4:30 AM every single day. My dad hated that bird. He used to joke about making chicken soup out of it. \n\nBut, uh, anyway... what was the question? Oh, right, something upsetting. Yeah, it was that confrontation with my boss two weeks ago. That's when all this jumpiness started.\n\n[Last Question]\nDid the anxiety and jumpiness start immediately after the confrontation with your regional boss, or was there a delay?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 27 / first_line_supervisor_of_non_retail_sales_worker / some_college\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, immediately after? I'm not really sure, honestly. It's kind of a blur. I just remember feeling so on edge after he left. It's weird how your body reacts to stuff like that, you know? Like adrenaline. It reminds me of this time I went white-water rafting with my college buddies. We went up to the Ocoee River in Tennessee. It was supposed to be this fun, bonding weekend, but the guide we got was this intense dude named River—I swear that was his real name, he showed us his license—and he had us paddling like we were in the Olympics. We hit this one rapid, I think they called it "Table Saw" or something terrifying like that, and the whole raft just completely flipped. I was underwater for what felt like ten minutes, but it was probably only five seconds. I swallowed so much river water. When I finally popped up, I was gasping, and my buddy Dave had lost his glasses. He's practically blind without them, so the rest of the trip he was just squinting at the trees thinking they were bears. We ended up having to buy him these ridiculous prescription goggles at a pharmacy on the drive back. He looked like a giant swimming insect. 

But, uh, sorry, what was I saying? Oh, right, when the jumpiness started. Yeah, I don't really know if it was right away or if it took a little bit. It all just kind of blends together after that night.

And when I finally got a hold of someone, the guy—I think his name was Gary—tried to tell me I owed a late fee. I was like, Gary, your system is the one that glitched! I had to escalate it to a manager just to get a $15 charge removed. It's the principle of the thing, you know? They just expect people to not notice and pay up. It's about this colony on Mars that loses contact with Earth, and the politics of how they survive. Anyway, I was in line for like twenty minutes because they only had three registers open. The guy in front of me had an entire cart full of just frozen mandarin orange chicken and two-buck Chuck. Um, within a few days? Yeah, I mean, like I said, this whole thing has been going on for about two weeks now. Ever since that late-night thing with my boss. It's just been a constant thing for the last two weeks. Two weeks is a long time to feel like this, honestly. It reminds me of this time I had to do jury duty. I should check the cereal aisle next time I'm at the grocery store. I usually just grab a protein bar on my way out the door now.

Just the being on edge and jumping at stuff.\n\nDoctor: Are you anxious all the time, even when you're not at work or in a situation that might be loud?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really all the time. Like, once I get home, I'm actually pretty okay. My apartment is usually pretty quiet. Well, except on Tuesdays when the garbage trucks come by. They do this massive recycling pickup and it clatters everywhere, but I know what it is, so it doesn't bother me as much. I actually had to call the city about them last month, though, because they kept leaving the empty bins right in the middle of my driveway. I was like, come on guys, I have to get to work. I drive this older Civic and it sits pretty low to the ground, so if I hit a plastic bin, it's definitely going to scratch the bumper. I just got it detailed, too, by this guy my buddy recommended. He did an amazing job on the upholstery, got these weird coffee stains out from the previous owner. I have no idea how you even spill coffee on the ceiling of a car, but he managed to scrub it right out... \n\nBut, uh, sorry. What were you asking? Oh, right, being anxious everywhere. No, I don't feel on edge when I'm just chilling at home or out running errands. It's really just when I'm at the office or if there's a bunch of sudden noise.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything in particular that you think is causing you to feel anxious at the office?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I guess... I mean, I don't really know if there's one specific thing causing it. It's just being there, I think. The whole environment. It's an open-plan office, which they said was going to foster \"collaboration\" when they remodeled it last year. But honestly, it's just a nightmare for trying to get any actual work done. There's no privacy at all. They put in these standing desks, which are supposed to be ergonomic, but the motors on them are so loud when people adjust them. And my desk is right by the breakroom, so I constantly hear the microwave beeping. We have this one guy, Todd, who microwaves fish at least twice a week. I've brought it up at the supervisor meetings—I'm like, guys, it's a basic office courtesy thing, we need a policy on pungent foods. But HR says we can't dictate what people eat for lunch. I get that, but when the whole floor smells like tilapia by 12:30, it really kills the vibe, you know? Plus, the fridge is always packed because people leave their Tupperware in there for weeks. I had to throw out a container of yogurt that expired in 2021 the other day. It was practically a science experiment. \n\nBut, uh, wait... what was the question? Something about the office? Yeah, I don't really know what's causing the anxiety there, specifically. I just feel super on edge the whole time I'm in the building.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about a wide range of things in your daily life, not just related to work or noise?\n\nPatient: Um, worry about a wide range of things? No, not really. I mean, I don't sit around stressing about everything under the sun. I pay my bills on time, mostly. Well, except for that one time last month when my auto-pay just mysteriously turned itself off for my internet bill. I use this provider, they're the only ones that service my building, and their customer service is just atrocious. I was on hold for like 45 minutes listening to this awful elevator jazz music. And when I finally got a hold of someone, the guy—I think his name was Gary—tried to tell me I owed a late fee. I was like, Gary, your system is the one that glitched! I had to escalate it to a manager just to get a $15 charge removed. It's the principle of the thing, you know? They just expect people to not notice and pay up. It's like how streaming services keep raising their prices by two dollars every few months hoping you won't cancel. I actually did cancel a bunch of them recently because I realized I was paying for like, four different platforms and only watching reruns of old sitcoms anyway. \n\nBut, uh, wait... what did you ask me? Oh, right, worrying about everything. No, I don't really worry about random daily stuff. It's really just the being jumpy at the office, like I said.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're alone with your thoughts, outside of work or noisy situations?\n\nPatient: Um, anxious when I'm alone with my thoughts? No, not really. When I'm just by myself, I'm usually fine. I actually kind of like the quiet. I try to read a lot when I'm alone. I just picked up this sci-fi series my brother recommended. It's about this colony on Mars that loses contact with Earth, and the politics of how they survive. It's super dense, though, like the author spends three pages just describing the hydroponic tomato farms. I tried growing tomatoes on my balcony last summer, actually. Total disaster. I bought these heirloom seeds online, got the special soil, even bought a little watering can that looked like a flamingo. But the squirrels in my neighborhood are just absolutely ruthless. They would wait until the tomatoes were perfectly red and ripe, and then just take one bite out of them and leave them on the railing to taunt me. I swear they were doing it on purpose. I tried putting cayenne pepper on the dirt because I read online that deters them, but then it rained and washed it all away, and I just gave up and went back to buying them at the farmer's market. There's this guy there who sells the best cherry tomatoes, anyway. \n\nBut, uh, sorry, what was I saying? Oh, right, being alone with my thoughts. Yeah, no, I don't really get anxious when I'm just sitting by myself or anything like that. It's just the work stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're in other crowded or public places, outside of your office?\n\nPatient: Um, crowded places outside the office? No, not really. I mean, I go to the grocery store and stuff and I'm totally fine. Actually, speaking of the grocery store, I was at Trader Joe's this weekend, and it was an absolute zoo. You know how their parking lots are always ridiculously small? I swear it's part of their business model to make you feel like you've accomplished something just by getting a spot. Anyway, I was in line for like twenty minutes because they only had three registers open. The guy in front of me had an entire cart full of just frozen mandarin orange chicken and two-buck Chuck. I mean, I like the orange chicken as much as the next guy, but that's a lot of sodium. I usually try to stick to the fresh produce section, even though their avocados are always either rock hard or complete mush. There is literally no in-between. I tried making guacamole last Tuesday for Taco Tuesday, which I do with my roommate, but the avocados were so bad I ended up just making salsa instead. I have a pretty good salsa recipe, actually. It uses roasted tomatillos. \n\nBut, uh, wait, what were we talking about? Crowded places? Yeah, no, I don't get anxious in crowds or public places. Just the office.\n\nDoctor: Has something specific and upsetting happened to you recently or in the past that you think might be contributing to how you're feeling now?\n\nPatient: Um, upsetting? Yeah, I guess so. This has all been going on for about two weeks, ever since this late-night confrontation I had with my regional boss. It was a whole thing. He came in way past normal hours, which he never does, and just started tearing into me about the quarterly numbers. And the thing is, the numbers weren't even finalized yet! Our software system, Salesforce, had been doing this weird update thing all week where it would duplicate leads and then randomly delete the notes attached to them. I spent like three hours on the phone with IT on Wednesday, and the IT guy—I think his name was Kevin, or maybe Kyle, he had this really thick Boston accent—was telling me that it was a known issue with the new patch they rolled out. They always roll out these patches without testing them properly, I swear. It's like when my phone forced an update overnight and suddenly all my alarm sounds changed to this weird harp music. I was late to work because who wakes up to a harp? It's too soothing. You need something jarring, like a buzzer or a rooster crowing. I actually used to live near a farm when I was a kid, and they had this rooster that would go off at 4:30 AM every single day. My dad hated that bird. He used to joke about making chicken soup out of it. \n\nBut, uh, anyway... what was the question? Oh, right, something upsetting. Yeah, it was that confrontation with my boss two weeks ago. That's when all this jumpiness started.\n\nDoctor: Did the anxiety and jumpiness start immediately after the confrontation with your regional boss, or was there a delay?\n\nPatient: Um, immediately after? I'm not really sure, honestly. It's kind of a blur. I just remember feeling so on edge after he left. It's weird how your body reacts to stuff like that, you know? Like adrenaline. It reminds me of this time I went white-water rafting with my college buddies. We went up to the Ocoee River in Tennessee. It was supposed to be this fun, bonding weekend, but the guide we got was this intense dude named River—I swear that was his real name, he showed us his license—and he had us paddling like we were in the Olympics. We hit this one rapid, I think they called it \"Table Saw\" or something terrifying like that, and the whole raft just completely flipped. I was underwater for what felt like ten minutes, but it was probably only five seconds. I swallowed so much river water. When I finally popped up, I was gasping, and my buddy Dave had lost his glasses. He's practically blind without them, so the rest of the trip he was just squinting at the trees thinking they were bears. We ended up having to buy him these ridiculous prescription goggles at a pharmacy on the drive back. He looked like a giant swimming insect. \n\nBut, uh, sorry, what was I saying? Oh, right, when the jumpiness started. Yeah, I don't really know if it was right away or if it took a little bit. It all just kind of blends together after that night.\n\nDoctor: Did the anxiety and jumpiness start immediately after or very shortly after the confrontation with your regional boss?\n\nPatient: Um, immediately after? Like I said, I'm really not sure. I mean, I don't know exactly when it kicked in. It's like when you get a cold, you know? You don't really know the exact second you got sick, you just wake up one day and your throat feels like sandpaper. I had this awful cold last winter, right around Christmas. It was the worst timing because I was supposed to go to my sister's place in Chicago. Have you ever been to Chicago in December? The wind literally hurts your face. They call it the Windy City, but I think they should call it the 'Frozen Tundra City.' Anyway, I had to cancel my flight, which was a nightmare because I flew with Spirit Airlines. Getting a refund from them is like trying to get blood from a stone. I spent hours on hold listening to this awful elevator music. It was this smooth jazz version of 'Girl from Ipanema' just looping over and over. I still hear it in my nightmares sometimes. I ended up just taking a flight credit that I still haven't used. I should probably go somewhere warm, like Florida. My buddy Dave—the one who lost his glasses in the river—he moved to Tampa last year. He says the humidity is brutal, but the beaches are nice. \n\nBut, uh, wait... what did you ask me? Right, when it started. Yeah, I honestly couldn't tell you if it was right that second or a bit later. I just don't know.\n\nDoctor: Did your anxiety and jumpiness start within a few days of the confrontation with your regional boss?\n\nPatient: Um, within a few days? Yeah, I mean, like I said, this whole thing has been going on for about two weeks now. Ever since that late-night thing with my boss. It's just been a constant thing for the last two weeks. Two weeks is a long time to feel like this, honestly. It reminds me of this time I had to do jury duty. I was stuck in this stuffy courthouse for exactly two weeks. It was a civil case, something about a property line dispute between these two neighbors who hated each other. One guy built a fence that was apparently three inches over the property line, and the other guy retaliated by planting these massive, ugly shrubs that blocked the first guy's view of the street. The lawyers were so boring, they just droned on and on about land surveys and municipal codes. I kept falling asleep in the jury box. The bailiff had to tap me on the shoulder twice. He was this older guy, looked exactly like Wilford Brimley, you know, the guy from those oatmeal commercials? 'It's the right thing to do and the tasty way to do it.' I used to eat that oatmeal every morning before school, the one with the little dinosaur eggs that hatch when you pour the hot water on them. I wonder if they still make those. I should check the cereal aisle next time I'm at the grocery store. I usually just grab a protein bar on my way out the door now. \n\nBut, uh... sorry, what were we talking about? Oh, right, the timing. Yeah, it's been about two weeks since the confrontation. That's how long this has been happening.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Um, within three months? Yeah, I mean, like I just said, it's only been about two weeks. Two weeks since that late-night thing with my boss. Three months is a long time. Three months ago... man, I think that's when I signed up for this meal kit delivery service. You know the ones? Where they send you the exact amount of spices in those tiny little plastic packets? It was supposed to save me time because managing the sales guys leaves me with zero energy to actually go grocery shopping. But honestly, the recipes were ridiculous. One of them wanted me to 'zest' a lemon. I don't even own a zester. I tried using a standard cheese grater and ended up scraping my knuckles up pretty bad. I literally bled right into the couscous. I had to throw the whole pan out and just order a pizza. And of course, the delivery guy forgot the garlic dipping sauce, which is honestly the only reason to order from that specific place. I tried to cancel the meal kit subscription the next day, but they make you click through like ten different screens of them begging you to stay before you can actually close the account. 

But, uh, anyway... sorry, I'm doing it again. What did you ask? Oh, right, three months. Yeah, no, like I was just saying, it hasn't been three months. It's just been going on for about two weeks, ever since that late-night confrontation with my regional boss.

Like I said, I'm really not sure. I mean, I don't know exactly when it kicked in. It's like when you get a cold, you know? Yeah, I honestly couldn't tell you if it was right that second or a bit later. Yeah, I mean, like I said, this whole thing has been going on for about two weeks now. Ever since that late-night thing with my boss. It's just been a constant thing for the last two weeks. Two weeks is a long time to feel like this, honestly. It reminds me of this time I had to do jury duty. I was stuck in this stuffy courthouse for exactly two weeks. It was a civil case, something about a property line dispute between these two neighbors who hated each other. One guy built a fence that was apparently three inches over the property line, and the other guy retaliated by planting these massive, ugly shrubs that blocked the first guy's view of the street. The lawyers were so boring, they just droned on and on about land surveys and municipal codes. I kept falling asleep in the jury box. The bailiff had to tap me on the shoulder twice. I keep telling our IT department—especially this guy Steve, who always takes his lunch right at 1:00 PM no matter what is currently on fire—I'm like, Steve, you can't just clock out when the server is lagging. They do this massive recycling pickup and it clatters everywhere, but I know what it is, so it doesn't bother me as much. I actually had to call the city about them last month, though, because they kept leaving the empty bins right in the middle of my driveway. I was like, come on guys, I have to get to work. I drive this older Civic and it sits pretty low to the ground, so if I hit a plastic bin, it's definitely going to scratch the bumper. I just got it detailed, too, by this guy my buddy recommended. He did an amazing job on the upholstery, got these weird coffee stains out from the previous owner. I mean, I don't really know if there's one specific thing causing it. It's just being there, I think. The whole environment. There's no privacy at all. They put in these standing desks, which are supposed to be ergonomic, but the motors on them are so loud when people adjust them. And my desk is right by the breakroom, so I constantly hear the microwave beeping. We have this one guy, Todd, who microwaves fish at least twice a week. I've brought it up at the supervisor meetings—I'm like, guys, it's a basic office courtesy thing, we need a policy on pungent foods. But HR says we can't dictate what people eat for lunch. I get that, but when the whole floor smells like tilapia by 12:30, it really kills the vibe, you know? Plus, the fridge is always packed because people leave their Tupperware in there for weeks. I had to throw out a container of yogurt that expired in 2021 the other day. It was practically a science experiment. \n\nBut, uh, wait... what was the question? Something about the office? Yeah, I don't really know what's causing the anxiety there, specifically. I just feel super on edge the whole time I'm in the building.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about a wide range of things in your daily life, not just related to work or noise?\n\nPatient: Um, worry about a wide range of things? No, not really. I mean, I don't sit around stressing about everything under the sun. I pay my bills on time, mostly. Well, except for that one time last month when my auto-pay just mysteriously turned itself off for my internet bill. I use this provider, they're the only ones that service my building, and their customer service is just atrocious. I was on hold for like 45 minutes listening to this awful elevator jazz music. And when I finally got a hold of someone, the guy—I think his name was Gary—tried to tell me I owed a late fee. I was like, Gary, your system is the one that glitched! I had to escalate it to a manager just to get a $15 charge removed. It's the principle of the thing, you know? They just expect people to not notice and pay up. It's like how streaming services keep raising their prices by two dollars every few months hoping you won't cancel. I actually did cancel a bunch of them recently because I realized I was paying for like, four different platforms and only watching reruns of old sitcoms anyway. \n\nBut, uh, wait... what did you ask me? Oh, right, worrying about everything. No, I don't really worry about random daily stuff. It's really just the being jumpy at the office, like I said.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're alone with your thoughts, outside of work or noisy situations?\n\nPatient: Um, anxious when I'm alone with my thoughts? No, not really. When I'm just by myself, I'm usually fine. I actually kind of like the quiet. I try to read a lot when I'm alone. I just picked up this sci-fi series my brother recommended. It's about this colony on Mars that loses contact with Earth, and the politics of how they survive. It's super dense, though, like the author spends three pages just describing the hydroponic tomato farms. I tried growing tomatoes on my balcony last summer, actually. Total disaster. I bought these heirloom seeds online, got the special soil, even bought a little watering can that looked like a flamingo. But the squirrels in my neighborhood are just absolutely ruthless. They would wait until the tomatoes were perfectly red and ripe, and then just take one bite out of them and leave them on the railing to taunt me. I swear they were doing it on purpose. I tried putting cayenne pepper on the dirt because I read online that deters them, but then it rained and washed it all away, and I just gave up and went back to buying them at the farmer's market. There's this guy there who sells the best cherry tomatoes, anyway. \n\nBut, uh, sorry, what was I saying? Oh, right, being alone with my thoughts. Yeah, no, I don't really get anxious when I'm just sitting by myself or anything like that. It's just the work stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're in other crowded or public places, outside of your office?\n\nPatient: Um, crowded places outside the office? No, not really. I mean, I go to the grocery store and stuff and I'm totally fine. Actually, speaking of the grocery store, I was at Trader Joe's this weekend, and it was an absolute zoo. You know how their parking lots are always ridiculously small? I swear it's part of their business model to make you feel like you've accomplished something just by getting a spot. Anyway, I was in line for like twenty minutes because they only had three registers open. The guy in front of me had an entire cart full of just frozen mandarin orange chicken and two-buck Chuck. I mean, I like the orange chicken as much as the next guy, but that's a lot of sodium. I usually try to stick to the fresh produce section, even though their avocados are always either rock hard or complete mush. There is literally no in-between. I tried making guacamole last Tuesday for Taco Tuesday, which I do with my roommate, but the avocados were so bad I ended up just making salsa instead. I have a pretty good salsa recipe, actually. It uses roasted tomatillos. \n\nBut, uh, wait, what were we talking about? Crowded places? Yeah, no, I don't get anxious in crowds or public places. Just the office.\n\nDoctor: Has something specific and upsetting happened to you recently or in the past that you think might be contributing to how you're feeling now?\n\nPatient: Um, upsetting? Yeah, I guess so. This has all been going on for about two weeks, ever since this late-night confrontation I had with my regional boss. It was a whole thing. He came in way past normal hours, which he never does, and just started tearing into me about the quarterly numbers. And the thing is, the numbers weren't even finalized yet! Our software system, Salesforce, had been doing this weird update thing all week where it would duplicate leads and then randomly delete the notes attached to them. I spent like three hours on the phone with IT on Wednesday, and the IT guy—I think his name was Kevin, or maybe Kyle, he had this really thick Boston accent—was telling me that it was a known issue with the new patch they rolled out. They always roll out these patches without testing them properly, I swear. It's like when my phone forced an update overnight and suddenly all my alarm sounds changed to this weird harp music. I was late to work because who wakes up to a harp? It's too soothing. You need something jarring, like a buzzer or a rooster crowing. I actually used to live near a farm when I was a kid, and they had this rooster that would go off at 4:30 AM every single day. My dad hated that bird. He used to joke about making chicken soup out of it. \n\nBut, uh, anyway... what was the question? Oh, right, something upsetting. Yeah, it was that confrontation with my boss two weeks ago. That's when all this jumpiness started.\n\nDoctor: Did the anxiety and jumpiness start immediately after the confrontation with your regional boss, or was there a delay?\n\nPatient: Um, immediately after? I'm not really sure, honestly. It's kind of a blur. I just remember feeling so on edge after he left. It's weird how your body reacts to stuff like that, you know? Like adrenaline. It reminds me of this time I went white-water rafting with my college buddies. We went up to the Ocoee River in Tennessee. It was supposed to be this fun, bonding weekend, but the guide we got was this intense dude named River—I swear that was his real name, he showed us his license—and he had us paddling like we were in the Olympics. We hit this one rapid, I think they called it \"Table Saw\" or something terrifying like that, and the whole raft just completely flipped. I was underwater for what felt like ten minutes, but it was probably only five seconds. I swallowed so much river water. When I finally popped up, I was gasping, and my buddy Dave had lost his glasses. He's practically blind without them, so the rest of the trip he was just squinting at the trees thinking they were bears. We ended up having to buy him these ridiculous prescription goggles at a pharmacy on the drive back. He looked like a giant swimming insect. \n\nBut, uh, sorry, what was I saying? Oh, right, when the jumpiness started. Yeah, I don't really know if it was right away or if it took a little bit. It all just kind of blends together after that night.\n\nDoctor: Did the anxiety and jumpiness start immediately after or very shortly after the confrontation with your regional boss?\n\nPatient: Um, immediately after? Like I said, I'm really not sure. I mean, I don't know exactly when it kicked in. It's like when you get a cold, you know? You don't really know the exact second you got sick, you just wake up one day and your throat feels like sandpaper. I had this awful cold last winter, right around Christmas. It was the worst timing because I was supposed to go to my sister's place in Chicago. Have you ever been to Chicago in December? The wind literally hurts your face. They call it the Windy City, but I think they should call it the 'Frozen Tundra City.' Anyway, I had to cancel my flight, which was a nightmare because I flew with Spirit Airlines. Getting a refund from them is like trying to get blood from a stone. I spent hours on hold listening to this awful elevator music. It was this smooth jazz version of 'Girl from Ipanema' just looping over and over. I still hear it in my nightmares sometimes. I ended up just taking a flight credit that I still haven't used. I should probably go somewhere warm, like Florida. My buddy Dave—the one who lost his glasses in the river—he moved to Tampa last year. He says the humidity is brutal, but the beaches are nice. \n\nBut, uh, wait... what did you ask me? Right, when it started. Yeah, I honestly couldn't tell you if it was right that second or a bit later. I just don't know.\n\nDoctor: Did your anxiety and jumpiness start within a few days of the confrontation with your regional boss?\n\nPatient: Um, within a few days? Yeah, I mean, like I said, this whole thing has been going on for about two weeks now. Ever since that late-night thing with my boss. It's just been a constant thing for the last two weeks. Two weeks is a long time to feel like this, honestly. It reminds me of this time I had to do jury duty. I was stuck in this stuffy courthouse for exactly two weeks. It was a civil case, something about a property line dispute between these two neighbors who hated each other. One guy built a fence that was apparently three inches over the property line, and the other guy retaliated by planting these massive, ugly shrubs that blocked the first guy's view of the street. The lawyers were so boring, they just droned on and on about land surveys and municipal codes. I kept falling asleep in the jury box. The bailiff had to tap me on the shoulder twice. He was this older guy, looked exactly like Wilford Brimley, you know, the guy from those oatmeal commercials? 'It's the right thing to do and the tasty way to do it.' I used to eat that oatmeal every morning before school, the one with the little dinosaur eggs that hatch when you pour the hot water on them. I wonder if they still make those. I should check the cereal aisle next time I'm at the grocery store. I usually just grab a protein bar on my way out the door now. \n\nBut, uh... sorry, what were we talking about? Oh, right, the timing. Yeah, it's been about two weeks since the confrontation. That's how long this has been happening.\n\nDoctor: Did your anxiety symptoms start within three months of the confrontation with your regional boss?\n\nPatient: Um, within three months? Yeah, I mean, like I just said, it's only been about two weeks. Two weeks since that late-night thing with my boss. Three months is a long time. Three months ago... man, I think that's when I signed up for this meal kit delivery service. You know the ones? Where they send you the exact amount of spices in those tiny little plastic packets? It was supposed to save me time because managing the sales guys leaves me with zero energy to actually go grocery shopping. But honestly, the recipes were ridiculous. One of them wanted me to 'zest' a lemon. I don't even own a zester. I tried using a standard cheese grater and ended up scraping my knuckles up pretty bad. I literally bled right into the couscous. I had to throw the whole pan out and just order a pizza. And of course, the delivery guy forgot the garlic dipping sauce, which is honestly the only reason to order from that specific place. I tried to cancel the meal kit subscription the next day, but they make you click through like ten different screens of them begging you to stay before you can actually close the account. \n\nBut, uh, anyway... sorry, I'm doing it again. What did you ask? Oh, right, three months. Yeah, no, like I was just saying, it hasn't been three months. It's just been going on for about two weeks, ever since that late-night confrontation with my regional boss.\n\nDoctor: Have your anxiety symptoms been present for less than six months?\n\nPatient: Less than six months? Yeah, definitely. I mean, like I keep saying, it's only been about two weeks. Six months is a really long time. Six months ago... man, I think that's when I bought that ridiculous smart mirror gym thing. You know the ones where the personal trainer is like, inside the glass, yelling at you to do more burpees? I was so convinced I was gonna get ripped. But now it just sits in my living room reflecting my couch. I tried doing a kettlebell swing the first week I had it and almost launched the weight right through my TV. And I love my TV, it's this massive 65-inch OLED. The picture quality is absolutely insane, especially when I'm playing PS5. I've been playing this massive open-world game lately, but honestly, it's exhausting. You start off trying to find some guy's stolen horse, and three hours later you're picking glowing mushrooms in a cave for a wizard. More than a month? No, no, I... I think I've said this a couple times now, but it's only been about two weeks. Just two weeks since that whole late-night thing with my regional boss. Less than six months but more than one month? Look, Doc, I feel like we're going in circles here. I keep telling you, it hasn't been a month. It hasn't been anywhere near that long. I usually try to stick to the fresh produce section, even though their avocados are always either rock hard or complete mush. There is literally no in-between. I tried making guacamole last Tuesday for Taco Tuesday, which I do with my roommate, but the avocados were so bad I ended up just making salsa instead. I have a pretty good salsa recipe, actually. It uses roasted tomatillos. \n\nBut, uh, wait, what were we talking about? Crowded places? Yeah, no, I don't get anxious in crowds or public places. Just the office.\n\nDoctor: Has something specific and upsetting happened to you recently or in the past that you think might be contributing to how you're feeling now?\n\nPatient: Um, upsetting? Yeah, I guess so. This has all been going on for about two weeks, ever since this late-night confrontation I had with my regional boss. It was a whole thing. He came in way past normal hours, which he never does, and just started tearing into me about the quarterly numbers. And the thing is, the numbers weren't even finalized yet! Our software system, Salesforce, had been doing this weird update thing all week where it would duplicate leads and then randomly delete the notes attached to them. I spent like three hours on the phone with IT on Wednesday, and the IT guy—I think his name was Kevin, or maybe Kyle, he had this really thick Boston accent—was telling me that it was a known issue with the new patch they rolled out. They always roll out these patches without testing them properly, I swear. It's like when my phone forced an update overnight and suddenly all my alarm sounds changed to this weird harp music. I was late to work because who wakes up to a harp? It's too soothing. You need something jarring, like a buzzer or a rooster crowing. I actually used to live near a farm when I was a kid, and they had this rooster that would go off at 4:30 AM every single day. My dad hated that bird. He used to joke about making chicken soup out of it. \n\nBut, uh, anyway... what was the question? Oh, right, something upsetting. Yeah, it was that confrontation with my boss two weeks ago. That's when all this jumpiness started.\n\nDoctor: Did the anxiety and jumpiness start immediately after the confrontation with your regional boss, or was there a delay?\n\nPatient: Um, immediately after? I'm not really sure, honestly. It's kind of a blur. I just remember feeling so on edge after he left. It's weird how your body reacts to stuff like that, you know? Like adrenaline. It reminds me of this time I went white-water rafting with my college buddies. We went up to the Ocoee River in Tennessee. It was supposed to be this fun, bonding weekend, but the guide we got was this intense dude named River—I swear that was his real name, he showed us his license—and he had us paddling like we were in the Olympics. More than two weeks but less than three months? Doc, I... I feel like I'm losing my mind here. We just went over this, right? Am I not making sense? It's been two weeks. Exactly two weeks. Not more, not less, just... about two weeks since that late-night confrontation with my regional boss. 

I don't know why we keep getting stuck on the exact same timeframe. It reminds me of this time I was trying to get my car inspected. I went to this place right near my apartment, and the guy told me my brake pads were worn down. So I paid him to replace them, right? But then the next day, my car starts making this horrible squealing noise every time I stop. Like a dying pig or something. So I take it back, and he tells me, 'Oh, the rotors are warped, you need new rotors.' And I'm like, why didn't you tell me that yesterday when you had the wheels off? So I pay for the rotors. I drive away. Two days later, the squealing is back. I take it back a third time, and this time a different mechanic looks at it and says the first guy put the brake pads on backwards. Backwards! How do you even do that? I had to argue with the manager for like forty-five minutes just to get them to fix it for free. And while I was waiting in the lobby, they had this TV playing nothing but infomercials for this weird hose that supposedly expands when you turn the water on. But it looked so flimsy, like it would just burst the second you actually tried to wash your car with it. 

Wait, what was I... sorry. I'm so sorry. I know I'm doing it again. I just get so worked up. What were you asking? Oh, the two weeks thing. Yeah, no, it hasn't been more than two weeks. Like I've been saying, it's just been about two weeks since the thing with my boss. Just two weeks.

It's about this colony on Mars that loses contact with Earth, and the politics of how they survive. It's super dense, though, like the author spends three pages just describing the hydroponic tomato farms. I tried growing tomatoes on my balcony last summer, actually. Total disaster. I bought these heirloom seeds online, got the special soil, even bought a little watering can that looked like a flamingo. But the squirrels in my neighborhood are just absolutely ruthless. They would wait until the tomatoes were perfectly red and ripe, and then just take one bite out of them and leave them on the railing to taunt me. I swear they were doing it on purpose. I tried putting cayenne pepper on the dirt because I read online that deters them, but then it rained and washed it all away, and I just gave up and went back to buying them at the farmer's market. There's this guy there who sells the best cherry tomatoes, anyway. \n\nBut, uh, sorry, what was I saying? Oh, right, being alone with my thoughts. Yeah, no, I don't really get anxious when I'm just sitting by myself or anything like that. It's just the work stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're in other crowded or public places, outside of your office?\n\nPatient: Um, crowded places outside the office? No, not really. I mean, I go to the grocery store and stuff and I'm totally fine. Actually, speaking of the grocery store, I was at Trader Joe's this weekend, and it was an absolute zoo. You know how their parking lots are always ridiculously small? I swear it's part of their business model to make you feel like you've accomplished something just by getting a spot. Anyway, I was in line for like twenty minutes because they only had three registers open. The guy in front of me had an entire cart full of just frozen mandarin orange chicken and two-buck Chuck. I mean, I like the orange chicken as much as the next guy, but that's a lot of sodium. I usually try to stick to the fresh produce section, even though their avocados are always either rock hard or complete mush. There is literally no in-between. I tried making guacamole last Tuesday for Taco Tuesday, which I do with my roommate, but the avocados were so bad I ended up just making salsa instead. I have a pretty good salsa recipe, actually. It uses roasted tomatillos. \n\nBut, uh, wait, what were we talking about? Crowded places? Yeah, no, I don't get anxious in crowds or public places. Just the office.\n\nDoctor: Has something specific and upsetting happened to you recently or in the past that you think might be contributing to how you're feeling now?\n\nPatient: Um, upsetting? Yeah, I guess so. This has all been going on for about two weeks, ever since this late-night confrontation I had with my regional boss. It was a whole thing. He came in way past normal hours, which he never does, and just started tearing into me about the quarterly numbers. And the thing is, the numbers weren't even finalized yet! Our software system, Salesforce, had been doing this weird update thing all week where it would duplicate leads and then randomly delete the notes attached to them. I spent like three hours on the phone with IT on Wednesday, and the IT guy—I think his name was Kevin, or maybe Kyle, he had this really thick Boston accent—was telling me that it was a known issue with the new patch they rolled out. They always roll out these patches without testing them properly, I swear. It's like when my phone forced an update overnight and suddenly all my alarm sounds changed to this weird harp music. I was late to work because who wakes up to a harp? It's too soothing. You need something jarring, like a buzzer or a rooster crowing. I actually used to live near a farm when I was a kid, and they had this rooster that would go off at 4:30 AM every single day. My dad hated that bird. He used to joke about making chicken soup out of it. \n\nBut, uh, anyway... what was the question? Oh, right, something upsetting. Yeah, it was that confrontation with my boss two weeks ago. That's when all this jumpiness started.\n\nDoctor: Did the anxiety and jumpiness start immediately after the confrontation with your regional boss, or was there a delay?\n\nPatient: Um, immediately after? I'm not really sure, honestly. It's kind of a blur. I just remember feeling so on edge after he left. It's weird how your body reacts to stuff like that, you know? Like adrenaline. It reminds me of this time I went white-water rafting with my college buddies. We went up to the Ocoee River in Tennessee. More than a month? Doc, seriously, are you even listening to me? I feel like I'm talking to a brick wall here. I keep saying the exact same thing. It hasn't been a month. It's been about two weeks. Two weeks since that late-night confrontation with my regional boss.

She said she was calling from Texas, and we ended up talking about how hot it gets down there compared to up here. 

But... wait. I'm doing it again, aren't I? I'm sorry. I just... I get so frustrated when I feel like I have to repeat myself. But yeah, to answer your question, no, it hasn't been more than a month. It's just been those two weeks since the thing with my boss.

Immediately after? Yeah, pretty much right away. Like I've been saying, it's been going on for about two weeks, ever since that late-night thing with him. It just started up right then and hasn't stopped.

Like, once I get home, I'm actually pretty okay. My apartment is usually pretty quiet. Well, except on Tuesdays when the garbage trucks come by. Well, except for that one time last month when my auto-pay just mysteriously turned itself off for my internet bill. I use this provider, they're the only ones that service my building, and their customer service is just atrocious. I was on hold for like 45 minutes listening to this awful elevator jazz music. And when I finally got a hold of someone, the guy—I think his name was Gary—tried to tell me I owed a late fee. I was like, Gary, your system is the one that glitched! I had to escalate it to a manager just to get a $15 charge removed. It's the principle of the thing, you know? They just expect people to not notice and pay up.
